{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_00_UQukeFDbsrQYFbZme9eF4317","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# SAT Environment: Boolean Satisfiability\n     2→# The model is given a CNF formula and must find a satisfying assignment.\n     3→# scorer(output) -> float in [-1.0, +1.0].\n     4→\n     5→import re\n     6→import random\n     7→from typing import Optional, List, Dict, Any\n     8→\n     9→from Gym.environment import VerifiableEnvironment\n    10→\n    11→\n    12→class SAT_Environment(VerifiableEnvironment):\n    13→    prompt_template = (\n    14→        'There are {N} boolean (0/1) values x[0], x[1], ..., x[{N_minus_1}]. '\n    15→        'Each of the following {M} expressions (`|` means OR, `!` means NOT) must equal 1:\\n'\n    16→        '{expressions}\\n\\n'\n    17→        'Please find any solution x[0], x[1], ..., x[{N_minus_1}] that satisfies '\n    18→        'the conditions above.\\n\\n'\n    19→        'Output Format: Your final answer should be a single line containing '\n    20→        'x[0], x[1], ..., x[{N_minus_1}], separated by **spaces**.\\n'\n    21→        'Example: `{N_boolean}` (do **NOT** include quotes or backticks).'\n    22→    )\n    23→\n    24→    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\n    25→        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n    26→        # Internal state set during _generate\n    27→        self.clauses: List[List[int]] = []       # each clause = list of literals\n    28→        self.reference_answer: List[int] = []    # the hidden satisfying assignment\n    29→\n    30→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    31→    #  Config (needed so verification can reconstruct the same problem)\n    32→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    33→\n    34→    def set_config(self, config: Dict) -> None:\n    35→        \"\"\"Restore config and regenerate the problem deterministically.\"\"\"\n    36→        super().set_config(config)\n    37→        # Regenerate the same problem using the stored seed\n    38→        self.generator(seed=self.seed, parameter=self.parameter)\n    39→\n    40→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    41→    #  Problem generation\n    42→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    43→\n    44→    def _generate(self) -> None:\n    45→        \"\"\"\n    46→        Generate a random satisfiable CNF formula.\n    47→\n    48→        self.parameter must contain:\n    49→          N       (int) – number of boolean variables\n    50→          M       (int) – number of clauses\n    51→          density (float, optional) – controls clause width;\n    52→                   k = max(2, min(N, 2 + round(density * N)))\n    53→        \"\"\"\n    54→        N = int(self.parameter[\"N\"])\n    55→        M = int(self.parameter[\"M\"])\n    56→        density = float(self.parameter.get(\"density\", 0.3))\n    57→\n    58→        # Clause width – density drives how many distinct variables appear per clause.\n    59→        k = max(2, min(N, 2 + round(density * N)))\n    60→\n    61→        # 1. Hidden satisfying assignment\n    62→        self.reference_answer = [random.randint(0, 1) for _ in range(N)]\n    63→\n    64→        # 2. Generate M clauses guaranteed to be satisfied by it\n    65→        self.clauses = []\n    66→        all_vars = list(range(N))\n    67→        for _ in range(M):\n    68→            clause = self._make_satisfied_clause(N, k, all_vars)\n    69→            self.clauses.append(clause)\n    70→\n    71→        # Persist for later stages\n    72→        self.parameter[\"reference_answer\"] = \" \".join(\n    73→            str(v) for v in self.reference_answer\n    74→        )\n    75→\n    76→    def _make_satisfied_clause(\n    77→        self, N: int, k: int, all_vars: List[int]\n    78→    ) -> List[int]:\n    79→        \"\"\"\n    80→        Return a clause (list of int literals) that *is* satisfied by\n    81→        self.reference_answer.\n    82→\n    83→        Literal encoding (1-indexed so sign works naturally):\n    84→          + (i+1)  →  x[i] == 1\n    85→          - (i+1)  →  x[i] == 0\n    86→        \"\"\"\n    87→        # Choose k distinct variables\n    88→        chosen = random.sample(all_vars, k)\n    89→\n    90→        # Random initial polarities\n    91→        clause = []\n    92→        for v in chosen:\n    93→            clause.append(v + 1 if random.random() < 0.5 else -(v + 1))\n    94→\n    95→        # If the clause is not satisfied, flip one random literal so it is.\n    96→        if not self._eval_clause(clause, self.reference_answer):\n    97→            idx = random.randrange(len(clause))\n    98→            var = abs(clause[idx]) - 1\n    99→            clause[idx] = var + 1 if self.reference_answer[var] == 1 else -(var + 1)\n   100→\n   101→        return clause\n   102→\n   103→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   104→    #  Prompt generation\n   105→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   106→\n   107→    def _prompt_generate(self) -> str:\n   108→        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n   109→        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n   110→\n   111→        lines = []\n   112→        for clause in self.clauses:\n   113→            parts = []\n   114→            for lit in clause:\n   115→                var = abs(lit) - 1\n   116→                parts.append(f\"x[{var}]\" if lit > 0 else f\"!x[{var}]\")\n   117→            lines.append(\"(\" + \" | \".join(parts) + \")\")\n   118→\n   119→        # Example output line (random, just to illustrate format)\n   120→        example = \" \".join(str(random.randint(0, 1)) for _ in range(N))\n   121→\n   122→        return self.prompt_template.format(\n   123→            N=N,\n   124→            N_minus_1=N - 1,\n   125→            M=M,\n   126→            expressions=\"\\n\".join(lines),\n   127→            N_boolean=example,\n   128→        )\n   129→\n   130→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   131→    #  Answer parsing\n   132→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   133→\n   134→    def _process(self, answer: Optional[str]) -> Optional[List[int]]:\n   135→        \"\"\"\n   136→        Parse the answer string (content between answer markers) into a\n   137→        list of 0/1 integers.  Returns None if parsing fails.\n   138→        \"\"\"\n   139→        if answer is None:\n   140→            return None\n   141→\n   142→        answer = answer.strip().strip('\"').strip(\"'\").strip(\"`\")\n   143→\n   144→        result: List[int] = []\n   145→        for token in answer.split():\n   146→            token = token.strip().rstrip(\",;\")\n   147→            try:\n   148→                v = int(token)\n   149→                if v in (0, 1):\n   150→                    result.append(v)\n   151→            except ValueError:\n   152→                continue\n   153→\n   154→        return result if result else None\n   155→\n   156→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   157→    #  Scoring\n   158→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n   159→\n   160→    def scorer(self, output: str) -> float:\n   161→        \"\"\"\n   162→        Score the model's raw output.\n   163→\n   164→        Returns a float in [-1.0, +1.0]:\n   165→          +1.0   every clause satisfied\n   166→          -1.0   parsing failure or zero clauses satisfied\n   167→          Linear interpolation otherwise:\n   168→              score = 2 * (satisfied / total) - '\n        'Each of the following {M} expressions (`|` means OR, `!` means NOT) must equal 1:\\n'\n        '{expressions}\\n\\n'\n        'Please find any solution x[0], x[1], ..., x[{N_minus_1}] that satisfies '\n        'the conditions above.\\n\\n'\n        'Output Format: Your final answer should be a single line containing '\n        'x[0], x[1], ..., x[{N_minus_1}], separated by **spaces**.\\n'\n        'Example: `{N_boolean}` (do **NOT** include quotes or backticks).'\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n        # Clause width – density drives how many distinct variables appear per clause.\n        k = max(2, min(N, 2 + round(density * N)))\n\n        # 1. Hidden satisfying assignment\n        self.reference_answer = [random.randint(0, 1) for _ in range(N)]\n\n        # 2. (i+1)  →  x[i] == 0\n        \"\"\"\n        # Choose k distinct variables\n        chosen = random.sample(all_vars, k)\n\n        # Random initial polarities\n        clause = []\n        for v in chosen:\n            clause.append(v + 1 if random.random() < 0.5 else -(v + 1))\n\n        # If the clause is not satisfied, flip one random literal so it is.\n        if not self._eval_clause(clause, self.reference_answer):\n            idx = random.randrange(len(clause))\n            var = abs(clause[idx]) - Returns None if parsing fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        if answer is None:\n            return None\n\n        answer = answer.strip().strip('\"').strip(\"'\").strip(\"`\")\n\n        result: List[int] = []\n        for token in answer.split():\n            token = token.strip().rstrip(\",;\")\n            try:\n                v = int(token)\n                if v in (0, 1):\n                    result.append(v)\n            except ValueError:\n                continue\n\n        return result if result else None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Scoring\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def scorer(self, output: str) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Score the model's raw output.\n\n        Returns a float in [-1.0, +1.0]:\n          +1.0   every clause satisfied\n          -1.0   parsing failure or zero clauses satisfied\n          Linear interpolation otherwise:\n              score = 2 * (satisfied / total) - The training reward improvement (+1.0 on SAT) is therefore a symptom of reward-specific overfitting, not genuine held-out learning.
